日本厚生劳动省报告称秋季流感季节异常提前 - 禽流感日记

Japan MHLW Reports Unusually Early Start to the Fall Flu Season - Avian Flu Diary
新闻简报日本厚生劳动省 (MHLW) 报告称,秋季流感季节异常提前,凸显了对中国出现新型 H3N8 禽流感病毒的担忧。该病毒于2024年在江苏省分离出来,源自候鸟,具有跨物种传播潜力。该研究于 2026 年 8 月 30 日发表,发现该病毒对鸟类和哺乳动物受体(包括人类上呼吸道中的受体)具有结合亲和力。这表明对人类健康存在潜在风险,因为该病毒可能已经适应了感染哺乳动物。研究还指出,该病毒是一种多重组病毒,结合了禽流感病毒各种亚型的基因片段。研究结果强调需要持续进行 监测 来监测病毒的传播和影响。该报告基于欧洲疾病预防和控制中心 (ECDC) 的证据,并在《跨界和新兴疾病》杂志上发表的一项研究中进行了详细介绍。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发表于2026年8月28日,事件日期为2026年8月31日。针对H3N8病毒的研究于2024年进行。
地点与对象
H3N8禽流感病毒于2024年在中国东部江苏省分离出来。据报道,该病毒具有跨物种传播潜力,并已在候鸟和家禽中发现。
事件
源自中国东部候鸟的多重组 H3N8 禽流感病毒表现出跨物种 传播 潜力。这是欧洲疾病预防和控制中心 (ECDC) 报告的,并在 2026 年 8 月 30 日发表的一项研究中详细介绍了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
该研究描述了一种具有双重受体结合能力和跨物种 传播 潜力的新型 H3N8 病毒的出现。它强调了由于该病毒能够与人类呼吸道受体结合的能力以及导致人类疾病的可能性,从而对人类健康构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
报告指出,该病毒已被分离和鉴定,需要继续监测。这些发现基于系统发育分析和实验研究,但对人类健康的全面影响仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Japan MHLW Reports Unusually Early Start to the Fall Flu Season - Avian Flu Diary

News Brief
Japan's Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare (MHLW) reported an unusually early start to the fall flu season, highlighting concerns about the emergence of a novel H3N8 avian influenza virus in China. This virus, isolated in Jiangsu Province in 2024, was derived from migratory birds and exhibits cross-species transmission potential. The study, published on August 30, 2026, found that the virus has a binding affinity for both avian and mammalian receptors, including those in the human upper respiratory tract. This suggests a potential risk to human health, as the virus may have adapted to infect mammals. The research also notes that the virus is a multirecombinant, combining genetic segments from various subtypes of avian influenza viruses. The findings emphasize the need for ongoing surveillance to monitor the virus's spread and impact. The report is based on evidence from the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and detailed in a study published in the journal 'Transboundary and Emerging Diseases'.
Original source text (English)
Avian Flu Diary Covering Pandemic and Seasonal Flu, Emerging Infectious Diseases, public health, community & Individual preparedness. NOTE: All AFD blogs are written by a human - any mistakes are solely mine. Monday, August 31, 2026 Transb. & Emerg. Dis.: A Multi-Reassortant H3N8 Avian Influenza Virus Derived From Migratory Birds in Eastern China Exhibits Cross-Species Transmission Potential Credit ECDC #19,316 While our knowledge of the subtypes of influenza pandemic viruses prior to 1918 is admittedly murky - historical and serological reconstructions (as depicted in the above pandemic timeline produced by ECDC) - suggest that an H3 virus, possibly H3N8, circulated globally around 1900. What is certain is that roughly seventy years later an avian H3 virus ressorted with seasonal H2N2 and produced another pandemic in 1968, and its descendents have remained in circulation for nearly 6 decades. This fits nicely into the H1, H2, H3 pattern of known influenza pandemic viruses going back nearly 140 years (see Are Influenza Pandemic Viruses Members Of An Exclusive Club?) Since then we've also seen: An equine H3N8 virus jump to dogs in 2004, followed three years later by an avian H3N2 virus in South Korea, with both reassorting with other subtypes (see J. Virology: Zoonotic Risk, Pathogenesis, and Transmission of Canine H3N2). An avian H3N8 virus was found in marine mammals (harbor seals) in 2011, and 2012’s mBio: A Mammalian Adapted H3N8 In Seals, provided evidence that this virus had recently adapted to bind to alpha 2,6 receptor cells, the type found in the human upper respiratory tract. In 2015's J.Virol.: Experimental Infectivity Of H3N8 In Swine, we saw a study that found that avian (but not canine or equine) H3N8 could easily infect pigs. All worrisome signals, but it was the 2022 emergence of a zoonotic H3N8 virus in China - first infecting 2 children 400 km apart in Henan and Hunan Provinces in April and May of 2022 - and more recently a fatal infection of a 56 year old woman in Guangdong Province, that really set off alarm bells. This led to 2023's EID Journal: Evolution of Avian Influenza Virus (H3) with Spillover into Humans, China, where researchers described finding 4 sub-lineages and an astonishing 126 genotypes of avian H3 viruses circulating in China. The authors also described a recent reassortment event where H3N8 acquired the internal genes from LPAI H9N2, a promiscuous virus which had previously contributed genes to both H5N1 and H7N9 (see PNAS: Evolution Of H9N2 And It’s Effect On The Genesis Of H7N9). Since then, we've seen an increasing number of cautionary reports on H3 viruses from China, including: J. of Infection: Global Spread of H3 Subtype Avian Influenza Viruses With an Accelerated Evolution After Interspecies Transmission Vet. Research: Emergence of a Novel Reassortant H3N3 Avian Influenza Virus with Enhanced Pathogenicity and Transmissibility in Chickens in China Transboundary & Emerg. Dis.: H3 Avian Influenza Virus Isolated from China in 2021–2022 Showed the Emerging H3N8 Posed a Threat to Human Health Eurosurveillance: Analysis of Avian Influenza A (H3N8) Viruses in Poultry and their Zoonotic Potential All of which serves as prelude to the following report - again from China - describing another migratory-bird-origin H3N8 subtype AIV - isolated and identified in Jiangsu Province in 2024 - that has also acquired a number of mammalian–adaptive mutations, exhibits dual receptor–binding capacity, and has cross-species transmission potential. Due to its length, and technical nature, I've only posted the link, abstract, and some excerpts from the discussion. Follow the link to read it in its entirety. I'll have a bit more after the break. A Multi-Reassortant H3N8 Avian Influenza Virus Derived From Migratory Birds in Eastern China Exhibits Cross-Species Transmission Potential Yunfei Guo, Zhonglong Yang, Hui Yang, Xinyu Miao, Tao Qing, Daxin Peng, Sujuan Chen First published: 30 August 2026 https://doi.org/10.1155/tbed/9889145Digital Object Identifier (DOI) PDF Abstract Migratory birds are one of the main reservoirs and long-distance transmission vectors of avian influenza viruses (AIVs). A migratory-bird-origin H3N8 subtype AIV (A/wild bird/Huadong/sy17/2024, hereafter named as WD/HDsy17/24) was isolated and identified in Jiangsu Province, Eastern China, in 2024. However, few studies have been conducted on the cross-species transmission potential of H3N8 AIVs from migratory birds. Phylogenetic analysis indicated that the eight gene segments of WD/HDsy17/24 originated from different subtypes of AIV such as H3N8, H1, H5N1, H5N2, and H7N7 and was a multirecombinant virus. WD/HDsy17/24 virus showed a binding affinity to both SAα-2, 3-galactose (Gal) and SA α-2, 6 Gal receptors, relatively weak thermal stability and pH stability. The SPF chicken pathogenicity indicated that the virus only causes mild respiratory symptoms and leads to lung hemorrhage and congestion. The mice pathogenicity indicated that the body weight of infected mice drops to the lowest value on the third day after infection, the lung-to-body ratio significantly increases, and inflammatory or edema lesions appear in the lungs. Moreover, this virus can infect guinea pigs through contact transmission and aerosol transmission routes from infected SPF chickens. After infection, obvious secretions can be seen in the eyes of guinea pigs. In the contact transmission group, viral shedding was detected in the nasal wash of one guinea pig at 6 days postinfection (dpi). In the aerosol transmission group, viral shedding was detected in the nasal washes of one guinea pig at both 10 and 12 dpi and in another guinea pig at 14 dpi. After 21 days, the seroconversion rate of serum antibodies in both groups is 100%. These findings underscore the need for continued surveillance of H3N8 viruses to identify circulating strains that may potentially threaten human health. (SNIP) Discussion As a natural reservoir, wild birds facilitate transregional and cross-national transmission [28]. H3N8 AIVs, a dominant subtype in wild birds, have a broad host range, infecting poultry [29] and crossing species barriers to infect horses, dogs, pigs, and humans [30–33]. Therefore, long-term surveillance in wild bird migration areas is urgently needed. Recent reports have documented novel reassortant H3N8 variants isolated a reassortant H3N8 from a wild bird in Jiangxi has become widespread in Chinese chicken flocks, forming a triple-reassortant genotype [5, 10]. The isolate WD/HDsy17/24 also displayed multi-reassortant characteristics, with eight gene segments derived from reassortment of H1, H3N8, H5N1, H5N2, and H7N7 influenza viruses. This is consistent with a broader analysis showing that wild-bird-origin H3N8 in China evolved into at least 126 genotypes from 2009 to 2022, with genotype G25 (reassorted with H9N2) capable of infecting chicken and crossing to humans [34]. The close genetic relationship of our isolate’s M and NS genes to H9N2 AIVs suggests enhanced host adaptability via reassortment. (SNIP) In summary, through analysis of the genetic evolutionary characteristics and pathogenicity of a wild-bird-origin H3N8 AIV from eastern China in 2024, this study confirmed that the strain carries multiple mammalian–adaptive mutations and exhibits dual receptor–binding capacity and cross-species transmission potential. The findings provide important experimental data for the early warning, surveillance, risk assessment, and control strategy development of H3N8 AIVs and are of great significance for mitigating their potential public health risks. (Continue . . .) While this latest H3N8 paper doesn't identify a new pandemic strain, it does add to what has become difficult to ignore; that the panoply avian H3 viruses in China have become increasingly diverse, they continue to evolve rapidly, and some have already displayed worrisome mammalian adaptations. As any kid with an erector set can tell you, the more interchangeable parts you have, the more neat stuff you can build. The same holds true for influenza reassortment - the more gene segments available for swapping - the more novel subtypes/genotypes that are possible. While that doesn't guarantee an untoward outcome, this trend has obviously captured the attention of Chinese researchers, and should be on our radar as well. Sunday, August 30, 2026 COVID Rates Low But Are Increasing Across The United States #19,315 Four weeks ago, in Mainland China, Taiwan & Hong Kong All Reporting Summer Surge of COVID, we looked at rising rates of COVID around the globe and the CDC's National Outlook which warned of a possible return visit later in the summer. The CDC's latest wastewater analysis now shows increasing COVID viral activity across much of the southern tier of states and the the west coast, and the overall epidemic trend is growing across the entire country (see below). None of this is to suggest we are seeing a major wave, but we are seeing an uptick. As the following CDC chart illustrates, while influenza and RSV have flatlined over the summer, COVID (in orange) is on the ascendent. Testing, and reporting, of COVID cases remain low, and so the CDC's Nowcast is unable provide us with much in the way of details on this summer's variants. This past week the FDA approved a new JN.1 lineage XFG subvariant COVID vaccine (see CIDRAP FDA approves new COVID vaccines) which should be available later this fall. Getting people to actually take the vaccine remains a challenge, particularly given the amount of anti-vaccine rhetoric on the internet. As the following CDC chart illustrates, uptake continues to decline. But having spent the past 2 decades chronicling the impact of viral infection (and reinfections) on individual health, I will be gladly rolling up my sleeve for both COVID and flu jabs again this fall. A few examples include: Eurosurveillance: Influenza Vaccination Attenuates Acute Myocardial Infarction and Stroke Risk Following Influenza Infection JAHA: Viral Infections and Risk of Cardiovascular Disease: Systematic Review and Meta‐Analysis Nature: Respiratory Viral Infections Awaken Metastatic Breast Cancer Cells in Lungs Nature: Viral Infections and the Risk of Neurodegenerative Diseases (Meta-Analysis & Systemic Review) CSIRO Pub: Impacts of Long COVID on Disability, Function and Quality of Life for Adults Living in Australia Saturday, August 29, 2026 mBio: Pre-existing Systemic and Nasal Antibodies Against Avian H5 Influenza A Viruses Vary According to Childhood Imprinting #19,314 Since H5Nx has never (as far as we know) circulated in the human population, the $64 question we keep returning to is whether there is any preexisting `immunity' in the general population against the virus - and if it exists - which community cohorts might have greater or lesser degrees of protection. This matters, because during the opening months of any novel flu pandemic, decisions would have to be made as to which groups to vaccinate with our expected limited supply of vaccines (see 2009's The Tracks Of Our Tiers). While influenza generally hits the oldest population the hardest, over the past 110 years we've seen at least 3 notable exceptions: The 1918 pandemic showed a unique W-Shaped Curve (see below), where young adults (25-30) were particularly hard hit, while mortality rate actually dropped in those over the age of 60. In 1978, H1N1 returned after an absence of 20 years (potentially from a lab leak in Russia or China) sparking a pseudo-pandemic (aka `the Russian Flu') that primarily affected those under the age of 20 (see mBio: The Reemergence Of H1N1 in 1977 and The GOF Debate). While the average (mean) age of a flu-related fatality in a `normal’ flu season here in the United States is about 76 years, the average during the (relatively mild) 2009 H1N1 pandemic was half that; at 37.4 years (see Study: Years Of Life Lost Due To 2009 Pandemic). Over the past 2 decades we've seen a lot work on the idea that the first flu HA type (Group 1 or 2) you are exposed to can shape your immune response to influenza for the rest of your life through a process called Original Antigenic Sin (OAS) (see PLoS Path.: Childhood Immune Imprinting to Influenza A). Those born prior to the mid-1960s were almost certainly first exposed to Group 1 flu viruses (H1N1 or H2N2) Those born after 1968 and before 1977 would have been exposed to Group 2 (H3N2) After 1977, both Group 1 and 2 viruses co-circulated, meaning the first exposure could have been to either one. But as we've seen repeatedly over the past 19,000+ blogs, nothing about our immune system is ever that simple; recent research has also found Cross reactive H5N1 Neuraminidase Antibodies vary by age and previous influenza A exposure as well. This suggests that any preexisting immunity is likely multilayered, and not automatically attributable to a single early exposure or age cohort. Complicating matters, laboratory evidence of preexisting H5Nx immunity is no guarantor of any real-world immunity. The oft postulated `immunity' of those born before 1957 would likely be minor, and might be more than offset by other age-related factors. While previous studies have found signs of systemic immunity in some cohorts, today's report in mBio also found that people born before 1957 had higher H5-reactive nasal IgG and IgA than later H3-imprinted groups. Again, the real world impact of having these immune responses is unknown. They may `recognize' the H5N1 virus, but their impact on an individual's outcome is far from certain. This is, nonetheless, a fascinating read. I've only reproduced the Abstract, and a few excerpts below. Follow the link to read it in its entirety. I'll have a brief postscript when you return. Pre-existing systemic and nasal antibodies against avian H5 influenza A viruses vary according to childhood imprinting Authors: Peta Edler , Kevin J. Selva , Ellie Reilly, Malet Aban, Ian G. Barr, Jennifer A. Juno , Adam K. Wheatley , Amy W. Chung, Stephen J. Kent , David J. Price , Marios Koutsakos marios.koutsakos@unimelb.edu.auAuthors Info & Affiliations https://doi.org/10.1128/mbio.01892-26 PDF/EPUB ABSTRACT Avian influenza A viruses (IAVs) pose a constant pandemic threat, with the recent 2.3.4.4b clade of the H5 subtype causing high pathogenicity and spreading across animal species and geographic locations. Understanding human pre-existing immunity to avian H5 IAV can inform on population susceptibility, a critical aspect of pandemic preparedness. To that end, we analyzed the IAV HA-specific antibodies across individuals born between 1928 and 1999 with different early life exposures to IAV subtypes. Individuals born prior to 1957 had the highest pre-existing serum antibodies to group 1 HA antigens, including the 2.3.4.4b H5 and a group 1 HA stem antigen. These birth year-specific patterns were not reflected in the limited pre-existing serum neutralizing antibodies detectable against a 2.3.4.4b H5 IAV or in H5-specific memory B cell populations. They were, however, evident in pre-existing nasal IgG and IgA titers to H5, which were greater in individuals born prior to 1957. Our findings demonstrate that the immunological biases afforded by early life exposure extend to antibodies detected in the nasal mucosa, the site of IAV replication. IMPORTANCE Understating pre-existing immunity to influenza A viruses (IAVs) of pandemic potential is an important aspect of pandemic preparedness. This includes an understanding of the heterogeneity of pre-existing immunity across the population. Here, we demonstrate that pre-existing antibodies to H5 IAV vary according to the year of birth and childhood imprinting. We demonstrate that this is the case for both systemic and nasal antibodies, highlighting the importance of understanding pre-existing mucosal immunity at the sites of influenza virus replication. (SNIP) DISCUSSION The spread of 2.3.4.4b H5 viruses across a plethora of animal species highlights the need for preparedness should this high-pathogenicity IAV acquire adaptations that support human-to-human transmission. An important aspect of pandemic preparedness is understanding pre-existing population immunity and heterogeneity in susceptibility. Here, we show that pre-existing antibodies against the avian H5 can be detected in adults, but their levels vary according to early life exposure to group 1 or group 2 HAs. We demonstrate that this does not translate into differences in serum neutralization activity or differences in circulating MBC pools, but it does result in differential levels of nasal IgG and IgA against H5. These findings have potential implications for the use of H5 vaccines as well as our understanding of immune imprinting. (Continue . . . ) During the last pandemic, COVID hit the elderly the hardest, but history has shown that influenza can - and occasionally does - target younger cohorts. Nearly 20 years ago, in A Predilection For The Young, I wrote about the disturbing skewing of H5N1 cases (and deaths) among younger individuals (see WHO Chart below). More recently (see here, and here) we've looked at the skewing of fatal H5N1 cases in Cambodia towards a younger cohort. Six years later we saw the opposite trend with avian H7N9 in China (an HA Group 2 virus), which skewed heavily toward older adults (see H7N9: The Riddle Of The Ages). While none of this can tell us exactly how an H5Nx pandemic might play out, the possibility of seeing a 1918-like age shift cannot be ignored. And even a relatively mild H5 pandemic that preferentially targets young adults or children would hit far differently than COVID. The report was published on August 28, 2026, and the event date is August 31, 2026. The study on the H3N8 virus was conducted in 2024.
Location and population
The H3N8 avian influenza virus was isolated in Jiangsu Province, Eastern China, in 2024. The virus has been reported to have cross-species transmission potential and has been found in migratory birds and poultry.
Event
A Multi-Reassortant H3N8 Avian Influenza Virus Derived From Migratory Birds in Eastern China Exhibits Cross-Species Transmission Potential. This was reported by the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and detailed in a study published on August 30, 2026.

Hong Kong students, at-risk residents can get flu jabs 1 week early this year - South China Morning Post

Hong Kong students, at-risk residents can get flu jabs 1 week early this year - South China Morning Post
新闻简报香港启动了 2026-27 年 季节性流感 疫苗接种 计划,该计划于 9 月 17 日开始,比上一年提前一周。政府多元化了 疫苗 采购工作,并简化了行政措施,以促进早期 疫苗 的实施。该计划针对的是在校学生和高危居民,包括65岁或以上的老年人、六个月至两岁的儿童、孕妇和患有慢性病的成年人。当局指出,虽然一些疫苗的提前到来使得接种活动得以启动,但老年居民的疫苗接种率仍然较低,为49.4%,与前三年的平均水平相比。政府预计新学年开始后学校会出现一些传播和暴发,并建议学生尽早接种疫苗。疫苗接种 补贴计划 (VSS) 以及针对学校和住宿护理院的外展计划是该计划的一部分。该政策包括电子同意书和医生在就诊期间的提醒,以推广 疫苗接种。来源是《南华早报》的全文,发表于 2026 年 8 月 31 日。
查看新闻五要素
时间
政府于今年早些时候启动了季节性流感 疫苗接种计划,从9月17日开始,比去年提前一周。该计划预计于 8 月底和 9 月初开始,部分 疫苗 最早将于 8 月底抵达。文章发表日期为2026年8月31日。
地点与对象
该政策适用于香港,特别针对在校学生和高危居民,包括65岁或以上的人士、六个月至两岁的儿童、孕妇和患有慢性病的成年人。
事件
香港推出了 2026-27 年 季节性流感 疫苗接种 计划,比上一年提前一周开始。政府对 疫苗 采购进行了多元化,并简化了行政措施,以促进早期 疫苗接种 的实施。该事件的确认基于官方声明和政策变化。
规模、影响与风险
该计划旨在提高学生和老年居民等弱势群体的 疫苗接种 率。根据政府数据,去年六个月至两岁儿童中有27.4%接种了疫苗,高于前三年19.9%的平均水平。但65岁及以上居民接种疫苗的比例仅为49.4%,比前三年平均水平低1.3个百分点。政府预计新学年开始后学校会出现一些传播和暴发,但没有报告具体的病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
政府已采取措施促进早期疫苗接种,包括电子同意书和医生在就诊期间的提醒。疫苗接种 计划正在进行中,当局正在审查该试点计划,以确定是否应该使其正常化。来源状态已满,文章提供了有关政策变更和疫苗采购的详细信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hong Kong students, at-risk residents can get flu jabs 1 week early this year - South China Morning Post

News Brief
Hong Kong has launched a seasonal influenza vaccination programme for 2026-27, beginning on September 17, one week earlier than the previous year. The government has diversified vaccine procurement efforts and streamlined administrative measures to facilitate early vaccination. The programme targets school students and at-risk residents, including those aged 65 or above, children aged six months to two years, pregnant women, and adults with chronic diseases. Authorities noted that while the early arrival of some vaccines enabled the start of the inoculation drive, the vaccination rate among elderly residents remains low at 49.4%, compared to the previous three-year average. The government expects some transmissions and outbreaks in schools after the new academic year starts and advises students to receive jabs as early as possible. The Vaccination Subsidy Scheme (VSS) and outreach programmes for schools and residential care homes are part of the initiative. The policy includes e-consent forms and reminders from doctors during medical encounters to promote vaccination. The source is a full article from the South China Morning Post, published on August 31, 2026.
Original source text (English)
Hong Kong school students and other at-risk residents can receive influenza vaccines one week earlier this year, after the government diversified its procurement efforts and streamlined administrative measures. Authorities also said on Monday they aimed to boost the injection rate among people aged 65 or above and children aged six months to two years, adding that they would rely on reminders from doctors during medical encounters as a prioritised measure. Dr Edwin Tsui Lok-kin, controller of the Centre for Health Protection, said the government’s seasonal influenza vaccination programmes for 2026-27 would begin on September 17, one week earlier than those in the previous year. “Influenza is still active in August despite it having slightly subsided from the peak, and as students gather after school starts, transmission will be quicker,” Tsui said. “We expect there will be some transmissions and outbreaks in schools after the new academic year starts, and therefore, we advise [students] to receive jabs as early as available.” The programmes include the Vaccination Subsidy Scheme (VSS), which offers subsidised doses to vulnerable groups such as children, residents aged 50 or above, pregnant women and adults with chronic diseases. View in App READ FULL ARTICLE The other covered schemes are the outreach vaccination programmes for schools and residential care homes. Tsui said the programmes could start early as the government had stepped up the diversification of vaccine procurement efforts. Dr Edwin Tsui has advised students to receive their jabs “as early as available”. Photo: Karma Lo Similar to last year, the government has bought injectable inactivated vaccines from French manufacturer Sanofi and Chinese pharmaceutical company Sinovac, nasal spray live attenuated vaccines from Swedish-British firm AstraZeneca and injectable recombinant jabs from Sanofi, totalling 1.3 million doses. This year, the number of Sanofi inactivated doses was reduced from 900,000 to 660,000, while the number from Sinovac was increased to 530,000, up from 300,000 in 2025. Meanwhile, the number of procured nasal spray and recombinant vaccines had increased to about 60,000 each, up from 50,000 each last year. Health authorities noted that while the Sanofi vaccines would only arrive in batches from mid-October, those from Sinovac had started arriving in late August. Tsui said the early arrival of some vaccines enabled authorities to start their inoculation drives earlier. All of this year’s procured vaccines are trivalent doses intended to protect against three flu strains. The World Health Organization earlier said the inclusion of the Yamagata variant of influenza B was no longer warranted. Tsui said the introduction of e-consent forms for parents through the government’s eHealth system had also allowed schools to make administrative preparations before the academic year started. Authorities also strengthened a pilot scheme that helped private doctors procure vaccines with government contracts by tripling the supply and expanding the eligibility for those taking part. Dr Christina Maw (left) and Tsui (second from right) join the fellow health officials at a press conference on Monday. Photo: Karma Lo Dr Christina Maw Kit-chee, assistant director of the Health Bureau’s Strategic Purchasing Office, said the government had procured 300,000 jabs under the scheme, up from 100,000 last year. The policy covers doctors who signed up for the VSS and at least one other government-subsidised primary healthcare programme. Last year’s programme was only open to those who joined both the VSS and the Chronic Disease Co-Care Pilot Scheme. Maw added that the government would review the pilot scheme and decide whether to normalise it after collecting information about demand levels from targeted groups as well as the ratio of government-procured vaccines to those doctors bought from private markets. According to government statistics, 2.03 million vaccines were administered under the government schemes in 2025-26, down from nearly 2.09 million a year earlier. Tsui noted that 27.4 per cent of children aged between six months and two years were covered by vaccines, higher than the moving average of the previous three years, 19.9 per cent, but the number would still need to rise as the proportion was relatively low. Government data also showed that only 49.4 per cent of residents aged 65 or above had received vaccines, 1.3 percentage points lower than the average of the previous three years. “From academic research, one of the most effective measures to promote such measures is [through] messages given by doctors during medical encounters in clinics,” Tsui said. He added that the government would work closely with all doctors to remind patients to get their flu jabs

Time
The government has started the seasonal influenza vaccination programme earlier this year, beginning on September 17, one week earlier than the previous year. The programme is set to begin in late August and early September, with some vaccines arriving as early as late August. The publication date of the article is August 31, 2026.
Location and population
The policy applies to Hong Kong, specifically targeting school students and at-risk residents, including those aged 65 or above, children aged six months to two years, pregnant women, and adults with chronic diseases.
Event
Hong Kong has launched a seasonal influenza vaccination programme for 2026-27, starting one week earlier than the previous year. The government has diversified vaccine procurement and streamlined administrative measures to facilitate early vaccination. The event is confirmed based on official statements and policy changes.
Scale, impact and risk
The programme aims to increase vaccination rates among vulnerable groups, including students and elderly residents. According to government data, 27.4% of children aged six months to two years were vaccinated last year, which is higher than the previous three-year average of 19.9%. However, only 49.4% of residents aged 65 or above were vaccinated, which is 1.3 percentage points lower than the previous three-year average. The government expects some transmissions and outbreaks in schools after the new academic year starts, but no specific numbers of cases or deaths are reported.
Response, status and uncertainty
The government has implemented measures to promote early vaccination, including e-consent forms and reminders from doctors during medical encounters. The vaccination programme is ongoing, and authorities are reviewing the pilot scheme to determine if it should be normalized. The source status is full, and the article provides detailed information about the policy changes and vaccine procurement.

印度甲型H1N1流感病毒感染病例激增属季节性,卫生官员称

India’s H1N1 Surge Is Seasonal, Health Officials Say
新闻简报印度正在经历甲型H1N1流感病毒感染的季节性激增,德里及多个邦报告病例增加。包括印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)在内的卫生官员确认,当前上升是预期季节性模式的一部分,未发现新的或异常危险的毒株。ICMR表示,流行的病毒为甲型密苏里/11/2025(H1N1)pdm09样毒株,属于已知系统发育谱系。约97%至98%的甲型流感检测为H1N1,98%至99%的病例为轻症且可自限。此次激增与印度常见的季风后流感流行期重叠,该国通常在1月至3月及季风或季风后数月出现两次高峰。印度卫生与家庭福利部正在监测疫情,当前无需过度担忧。卫生官员建议,轻症患者无需进行检测或住院,如症状持续或加重,应遵循医嘱。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据报道,H1N1 病例激增是季节性的,在 2026 年 8 月季风后期间,德里和其他邦的感染病例有所增加。目前的情况正在作为正在进行的 监测 的一部分进行监测。
地点与对象
印度,具体包括德里及多个邦(如卡纳塔克邦、马哈拉施特拉邦、喀拉拉邦等)。受影响人群包括呼吸道感染患者,重点关注高风险人群。
事件
印度正在经历甲型H1N1流感病毒感染的季节性激增,卫生官员确认当前病例增加属于预期季节性模式。ICMR表示未发现新的或异常危险的毒株,确认流行病毒为甲型密苏里/11/2025(H1N1)pdm09样毒株。
规模、影响与风险
在印度,H1N1约占甲型流感检测的97%至98%,德里单地实验室确诊病例已达1,777例。ICMR估计98%至99%的病例为轻症且可自限,通过支持性治疗即可控制。未发现新的危险毒株,风险被视为正常季节性变异。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

India’s H1N1 Surge Is Seasonal, Health Officials Say

News Brief
India is experiencing a seasonal surge in H1N1 infections, with Delhi and several other states reporting increased cases. Health officials, including the ICMR, have confirmed that the current rise is part of the expected seasonal pattern, with no new or unusually dangerous strain identified. The ICMR states that the circulating virus is an A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like strain, belonging to a recognised lineage. Approximately 97% to 98% of Influenza A detections are H1N1, with 98% to 99% of cases being mild and self-limiting. The surge coincides with the post-monsoon influenza period, which is common in India, with two peaks typically occurring between January-March and during monsoon or post-monsoon months. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare is monitoring the situation, and there is no cause for alarm. Health officials have advised that mild illness does not necessarily require testing or hospitalisation, and individuals should follow medical advice if symptoms persist or worsen.
Original source text (English)

MEDINDIA MEDINDIA Consult Online Doctor Courses Explore Healthy Living News Health A-Z Calculators Articles Drugs Directories Education More Search Medindia Medindia » News » Environmental Health » India's H1N1 Surge Is Seasonal, Health Officials Say India's H1N1 Surge Is Seasonal, Health Officials Say Written by Shravanthi Vikram Reviewed by The Medindia News Review Team on Aug 26 2026 1:41 PM Busy? Tap to listen instead of reading 0:00/0:00 H1N1 is driving India’s current seasonal influenza surge, with Delhi and several other states reporting increased infections. ICMR says no new or unusually dangerous strain has emerged, but high-risk individuals should watch for warning signs and seek timely medical advice. India is experiencing a seasonal increase in respiratory infections, with Influenza A (H1N1)pdm09 emerging as the dominant influenza subtype in current surveillance. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare says the situation is being continuously monitored by the Indian Council of Medical Research, Department of Health Research and National Centre for Disease Control. ICMR Director General Dr Rajiv Bahl said there was “no cause for panic or alarm,” because the circulating influenza viruses follow patterns observed during previous seasonal waves. Reports from the briefing indicate that H1N1 accounts for approximately 97% to 98% of Influenza A detections, while H3N2 represents a much smaller proportion. Influenza B viruses and low levels of SARS-CoV-2 are also circulating. Although infection numbers have increased, ICMR estimates that approximately 98% to 99% of cases are mild, self-limiting and manageable with supportive care. Preventing Flu: Good Health Habits Can Help Stop Germs How is the flu spread and how effective is flu treatment? Learn more about the influenza virus and get the best tips and info on flu prevention. No New or Unusually Dangerous H1N1 Strain Was Found Genomic surveillance identified the circulating virus as an A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like strain. It belongs to a recognised viral lineage that has been circulating in India rather than representing a newly emerged strain. Influenza viruses undergo small genetic changes called antigenic drift as they spread. These gradual mutations can allow the virus to infect people who previously had influenza, contributing to recurring seasonal waves. However, normal antigenic drift is different from the major genetic change that can produce a new pandemic virus. ICMR said the changes detected in the current H1N1 samples were expected and did not indicate a more dangerous variant. H1N1 is still commonly called “swine flu” because of the origins of the virus responsible for the 2009 pandemic. The pdm09 virus has since become established as a seasonal human influenza strain that circulates mainly from person to person. Delhi Reports a Sharp Increase in H1N1 Cases Delhi recorded 2,602 laboratory-confirmed Influenza A and B cases through August 20, 2026, according to government data. Of 2,392 Influenza A infections, 1,777 were classified as H1N1. The pattern differs from 2025, when H3N2 was the predominant influenza subtype in the capital. Increased influenza activity has also been reported in Karnataka, Maharashtra, Kerala and other states. These rises coincide with India’s post-monsoon influenza period. Seasonal influenza activity in India commonly has two peaks: one between January and March and another during the monsoon or post-monsoon months. A rise during these periods does not necessarily indicate a new outbreak strain, although sustained surveillance remains important.(1✔ ✔Trusted Source Seasonal influenza - Global situation Go to source) Union Health Minister J.P. Nadda reviewed national and Delhi preparedness on August 21. The review covered hospital beds, intensive-care capacity, oxygen support, ventilators, diagnostic services, medicines and referral arrangements for patients requiring specialised care. Swine Flu Swine flu, a type of influenza caused by a new strain of the H1N1 Type A influenza virus has originated from the pigs. Winter always brings along the chills and the flu, make sure you''re safe this season. Common H1N1 Symptoms to Watch For H1N1 causes symptoms similar to those of other seasonal influenza viruses. Illness often begins more abruptly than a common cold. Common symptoms include: Fever or chills Dry or persistent cough Sore throat Runny or blocked nose Headache Muscle and body aches Fatigue or weakness Reduced appetite Vomiting and diarrhoea may occur, particularly among children. Not everyone with influenza develops a fever, and symptoms alone cannot reliably distinguish H1N1 from H3N2, Influenza B, COVID-19 or another respiratory infection.(2✔ ✔Trusted Source Signs and Symptoms of Flu Go to source) Most otherwise healthy people recover with rest, adequate fluids and symptom management. Mild illness does not automatically require influenza testing, hospitalisation or antiviral treatment. People who are unwell should stay home, when possible, avoid close contact with others and follow medical advice if symptoms persist or worsen. Delhi H1N1 Alert: When Flu Symptoms Need Urgent Care Delhi’s H1N1 tally has reached 1,777, prompting advice on masks, hygiene, vaccination and warning signs that require medical care. Warning Signs Require Prompt Medical Attention Most H1N1 infections are mild, but breathing difficulty, chest pain, bluish skin, confusion, seizures, dehydration, coughing up blood or symptoms that worsen after 72 hours require prompt medical assessment. Parents should seek care for children with poor feeding, unusual drowsiness or breathing problems. Because influenza can resemble dengue, COVID-19 and other illnesses, severe symptoms should not be treated with home remedies alone. Delhi H1N1 Cases Reach 1,777 Amid Flu Surge Delhi has recorded 1,777 H1N1 cases in 2026, prompting a review of hospital readiness, surveillance and public precautions. Who Faces a Higher Risk of H1N1 Complications? Older adults, children under five, particularly those younger than two and pregnant or recently postpartum women face a greater risk of severe influenza. The risk is also higher among people with asthma, diabetes, severe obesity, weakened immunity or chronic heart, lung, kidney, liver or neurological conditions, as well as cancer and transplant patients. Being in a high-risk group does not mean severe illness is inevitable. However, symptoms should be monitored carefully, and medical advice should be sought promptly because timely antiviral treatment may reduce the risk of complications in appropriate patients. Universal Masking Is Not Being Recommended ICMR has not recommended routine mask use for the entire healthy population during the current seasonal increase. Instead, it emphasises staying home when ill, washing hands, covering coughs, improving ventilation and avoiding close contact with symptomatic people. Masks can still be useful for infected individuals, caregivers, healthcare workers, close household contacts and high-risk people in crowded indoor settings. They should fit securely and disposable masks should be replaced when wet, damaged or soiled. Flu Vaccination Is Most Important for High-Risk Groups Seasonal influenza vaccination is not part of India’s universal immunisation programme, but older adults, pregnant women, healthcare workers and people with chronic diseases or weakened immunity should discuss it with their doctors. The circulating H1N1, H3N2 and Influenza B viruses broadly match the WHO-recommended Northern Hemisphere vaccine composition for 2026–27.(1✔ ✔Trusted Source Seasonal influenza - Global situation Go to source) Vaccination may not prevent every infection, but it can lower the risk of severe illness, hospitalisation and complications. Antivirals Require Medical Supervision The influenza viruses tested through national surveillance remain sensitive to oseltamivir. However, the medicine should be used only when prescribed, particularly for severe, progressive or complicated infections and selected high-risk patients. Most mild cases improve with rest, hydration and symptom relief. Antibiotics do not treat H1N1 and should be used only when a clinician suspects a secondary bacterial infection. India Is Tracking Influenza in Real Time India monitors respiratory infections through 73 DHR-ICMR sentinel sites and 167 Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratories. The network tests samples for H1N1, H3N2, Influenza B, SARS-CoV-2 and several other respiratory viruses. Current surveillance has identified a familiar seasonal H1N1 strain rather than a new pandemic virus. Authorities continue tracking genetic changes, disease severity, hospital demand and unusual clusters. Rising H1N1 Cases Call for Caution, Not Alarm The rise in H1N1 cases requires awareness and healthcare preparedness, but current evidence does not indicate a new or unusually dangerous strain. People at increased risk should be particularly attentive to symptoms and discuss influenza vaccination with a qualified healthcare professional. The central message is balanced: rising H1N1 cases should not be ignored, but they should not be treated as evidence of another pandemic. Timely care, accurate information and protection of vulnerable individuals remain the most effective response. References: Seasonal influenza - Global situation- (https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2025-DON586) Signs and Symptoms of Flu- (https://www.cdc.gov/flu/signs-symptoms/index.html) Source-Medindia TOP INSIGHT Did You Know? 学生返校时卫生防护中心发出流感 暴发 警告 - 英文虎报

CHP warns of flu outbreaks as students head back to school - The Standard (HK)
新闻简报香港卫生防护中心(CHP)发出警告,预计随着学生返校,流感活动可能会增加。卫生防护中心表示,自六月下旬流感季节开始以来,已录得11宗严重小儿流感个案。其中,2名患者情况危急,1人死亡。卫生防护中心的最新数据显示流感活动频繁,引发了人们对学校可能出现 暴发 的担忧。虽然Covid-19活动自8月初高峰以来已呈下降趋势,且自6月以来已录得两例严重儿科病例,但卫生防护中心强调,香港仍处于流感季节。卫生防护中心总监徐乐坚敦促学校和家长参与2026/27年度季节性流感 疫苗接种学校外展计划,强调疫苗接种是最好的预防措施。该警告强调了由于儿童之间的密切接触而导致流感在学校环境中传播的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
自六月底进入流感季节以来,卫生防护中心已录得 11 宗严重小儿流感感染个案。与此同时,自 8 月初的峰值以来,Covid-19 活动呈下降趋势,自 6 月以来记录了两例严重儿科病例。卫生防护中心总监徐乐健强调,香港仍处于流感季节,预计新学年开始后病毒仍将活跃。

规模、影响与风险
据了解,两名患者均未接种疫苗。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

CHP warns of flu outbreaks as students head back to school - The Standard (HK)

News Brief
The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) in Hong Kong has issued a warning about the anticipated rise in influenza activity as students return to school. According to the CHP, 11 severe paediatric influenza cases have been recorded since the start of the influenza season in late June. Among these, two patients remain in critical condition, and one has died. The CHP's latest data indicates high flu activity, prompting concerns about potential school outbreaks. While there has been a downward trend in Covid-19 activity since its peak in early August, with two severe paediatric cases recorded since June, the CHP emphasized that Hong Kong is still in the midst of the influenza season. The CHP Controller, Edwin Tsui Lok-kin, urged schools and parents to participate in the 2026/27 Seasonal Influenza Vaccination School Outreach Programme, highlighting vaccination as the best prevention. The warning underscores the risk of flu spread in school settings due to close contact among children.

Osivax 与 GC Biopharma 合作开发结合 OVX836 和 GC FLU 的下一代流感 疫苗 候选药物

Osivax, GC Biopharma collaborate to develop a next-generation influenza vaccine candidate combining OVX836 and GC FLU
新闻简报2026年8月29日,Osivax和GC Biopharma宣布建立长期合作,开发结合OVX836和GC FLU的下一代季节性流感 疫苗候选药物。此次合作旨在开发一种单次注射剂 疫苗,利用互补免疫机制,针对循环和新出现的流感病毒株提供更广泛、更持久的保护。此次合作基于 Osivax 针对保守核蛋白 (NP) 抗原的 T 细胞聚焦方法和 GC Biopharma 的标准剂量、经 WHO 资格预审的灭活 季节性流感 疫苗、GC FLU。疫苗 组合预计将解决 季节性流感 带来的重大公共卫生负担,特别是老年人和高危人群。Osivax 将领导韩国以外的全球开发和商业化,GC Biopharma 保留韩国的独家权利。该协议支持 GC Biopharma 向先进市场扩张,同时巩固其国内地位。两家公司尚未披露额外的财务条款,疫苗 仍处于开发阶段。该举措强调了通过创新的 疫苗 战略改善流感预防的持续努力。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Osivax, GC Biopharma collaborate to develop a next-generation influenza vaccine candidate combining OVX836 and GC FLU

News Brief
On August 29, 2026, Osivax and GC Biopharma announced a long-term collaboration to develop a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine candidate combining OVX836 and GC FLU. This partnership aims to create a single injection vaccine that leverages complementary immune mechanisms to provide broader and more durable protection against circulating and emerging influenza strains. The collaboration is based on Osivax’s T-cell-focused approach targeting the conserved nucleoprotein (NP) antigen and GC Biopharma’s standard-dose, WHO-prequalified inactivated seasonal influenza vaccine, GC FLU. The combination vaccine is expected to address the significant public health burden of seasonal influenza, especially among older adults and high-risk individuals. Osivax will lead global development and commercialization outside Korea, where GC Biopharma retains exclusive rights. The agreement supports GC Biopharma's expansion into advanced markets while reinforcing its domestic position. The companies have not disclosed additional financial terms, and the vaccine remains in the development phase. This initiative highlights the ongoing efforts to improve influenza prevention through innovative vaccine strategies.
Original source text (English)

BODIES Govt Recognised In-House R&D Units AICTE Boards & Committees List of Indian CROs List of CROs (International) Govt & approved labs of India List of State Drugs Controllers Join Pharma | Login Home > News you can get e-magazine links on WhatsApp. Click here Biotechnology + Font Resize - Osivax, GC Biopharma collaborate to develop a next-generation influenza vaccine candidate combining OVX836 and GC FLU Lyon, France Saturday, August 29, 2026, 16:00 Hrs [IST] Osivax, a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company developing differentiated influenza vaccines designed to generate broad-spectrum immune responses, and GC Biopharma, a leading South Korean biopharmaceutical company with extensive experience in vaccines and plasma-derived products, announced a long-term collaboration to develop and commercialize a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine candidate designed to provide broader and more durable protection against a wide range of influenza strains. Under the agreement, the companies will develop the combination of OVX836, Osivax's broad-spectrum influenza A vaccine candidate targeting the highly conserved nucleoprotein (NP), with GC FLU, GC Biopharma's standard-dose, WHO-prequalified, inactivated seasonal influenza vaccine, in a single injection. The combination is expected to leverage complementary immune mechanisms to provide higher and broader protection against circulating and emerging influenza strains. OVX836 is designed to induce T-cell responses against the highly conserved NP antigen, while GC FLU provides antibody responses against the well-known Hemagglutinin (HA) surface antigen. The agreement grants Osivax a non-exclusive, sublicensable, global license (excluding Japan and Korea) to use GC FLU in combination with OVX836. Osivax will lead the global development strategy for the combination vaccine candidate and will be responsible for its development and commercialization outside the Republic of Korea, where GC Biopharma will retain exclusive rights. GC Biopharma will receive royalties and ensure long-term supply of GC FLU. Additional details and financial terms have not been disclosed. “Despite decades of progress in influenza vaccination, there remains a significant need for approaches that can provide broader and more durable protection against evolving influenza strains,” said Alexandre Le Vert, CEO and co-founder of Osivax. “This agreement marks a key step towards developing a superior seasonal influenza vaccine by combining Osivax’s T-cell-focused approach with GC Biopharma’s established GC FLU vaccine and manufacturing capabilities. This partnership is a cornerstone of our strategy to lead the development of a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine while preserving Osivax’s strategic independence.” "Advancing next-generation influenza vaccines requires collaboration between companies with complementary expertise across various scientific disciplines," said Eun-Chul Huh, CEO of GC Biopharma. "We are pleased to partner with Osivax to develop a combination vaccine candidate designed to address long-standing unmet needs in seasonal influenza prevention. This agreement supports GC Biopharma's continued expansion into major advanced markets while reinforcing our domestic position." Seasonal influenza remains a significant public health burden, particularly among older adults and people at increased risk of complications. Current seasonal influenza vaccines primarily target surface antigens that can vary substantially from season to season. OVX836 is designed to target NP, a more conserved internal influenza A antigen, with the aim of broadening protection across influenza A strains. Osivax previously reported that co-administration of OVX836 with a seasonal inactivated influenza vaccine was well tolerated and induced complementary immune responses, supporting further development of combination approaches. OVX836 is a first-in-class influenza A vaccine candidate that targets the nucleoprotein (NP), a highly conserved internal antigen. Unlike surface antigens, the NP is much less likely to mutate, providing a broader and more universal immune response. Osivax’ oligoDOM technology enables the design and production of a recombinant version of the NP, which self-assembles into a nanoparticle, thus triggering powerful T- and B-cell immune responses. OVX836 has been tested in 7 clinical trials with over 4,000 participants so far, and has shown promising safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy read-outs. GC FLU is GC Biopharma's seasonal influenza vaccine. WHO prequalification listings identify GC FLU inj. and GC FLU Multi inj. as current WHO-prequalified seasonal trivalent influenza vaccines manufactured by GC Biopharma Corp. in the Republic of Korea. Osivax’s lead candidate, OVX836, is a broad-spectrum influenza A vaccine candidate currently in Phase 2b clinical trials with over 4,000 volunteers tested and encouraging efficacy proof of concept data. By combining OVX836 with standard flu vaccines, Osivax is advancing toward a best-in-class seasonal flu vaccine to prevent all strains of influenza. Osivax collaborates with leading global health agencies, including US based BARDA and NIH, and European-based institutions and top-tier pharmaceutical companies, reinforcing its mission to prevent all types of seasonal and pandemic influenza. The company will expand into other infectious disease indications through combinations and collaborations worldwide. GC Biopharma has more than half a century of experience in the development and manufacturing of plasma derivatives and vaccines, and is expanding its global presence. GC Biopharma also continues to invest in innovation by leveraging R&D capabilities in protein engineering, mRNA and lipid nanoparticle drug delivery technologies to develop therapeutics in areas including rare diseases and immunology and inflammation. Health Min.: VACSERA to provide over 300K doses of Influenza vaccine - Egypt Today
新闻简报埃及卫生与人口部于2026年8月29日宣布,VACSERA将提供超过300,000剂季节性流感 疫苗(包括三价和四价型,由赛诺菲等制造商生产),以满足公众需求。分发工作将于2026年9月1日启动,通过全国28个中心进行,目标人群为在校儿童、老年人、慢性病患者、孕妇及医护工作者。该举措旨在减少流感病毒传播、降低肺炎风险,并减少住院或重症监护需求。埃及卫生部强调,疫苗接种在冬季增强免疫力方面至关重要,并敦促公众在每个流感季节开始时接种疫苗,因病毒株存在持续的进化。公共宣传活动(如“#TogetherWeAreReassured”)强调,尽管疫苗无法完全预防感染,但可显著降低接种者症状的严重程度。

时间
埃及卫生和人口部宣布将于 2026 年 8 月 29 日推出 季节性流感 疫苗,并于 2026 年 9 月 1 日开始分发。
地点与对象
该倡议以埃及为重点,针对全国公民,特别是学校和拥挤场所的儿童,以及老年人、慢性病患者、孕妇和医护人员。

应对、状态与不确定性
卫生和人口部致力于从 2026 年 9 月 1 日起通过其全国 28 个中心分发 疫苗,并提高公众对 疫苗 在降低感染严重程度方面作用的认识。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Health Min.: VACSERA to provide over 300K doses of Influenza vaccine - Egypt Today

News Brief
The Egyptian Ministry of Health and Population announced on 29 August 2026 that VACSERA will provide over 300,000 doses of seasonal influenza vaccines, including trivalent and quadrivalent types from manufacturers such as Sanofi, to meet public demand. Distribution will begin on 1 September 2026 through 28 centers across the country, targeting children in schools, the elderly, individuals with chronic illnesses, pregnant women, and healthcare workers. The initiative aims to reduce the spread of influenza viruses, lower the risk of pneumonia, and decrease the need for hospitalization or intensive care. The Ministry emphasized that vaccination is crucial for boosting immunity during the winter season and urged citizens to get vaccinated at the start of each flu season due to the constant evolution of virus strains. Public awareness campaigns, such as "#TogetherWeAreReassured," highlight that while the vaccine does not prevent infection entirely, it significantly reduces the severity of symptoms in vaccinated individuals.
Original source text (English)
News Business Arts & Culture Our Magazine Sports ET Scenes Haya Karima News Business Arts & Culture ET Scenes Our magazine Sports Health Min.: VACSERA to provide over 300K doses of Influenza vaccine BY Egypt Today staff - Sat, 29 Aug 2026 - 08:27 GMT BY Egypt Today staff Sat, 29 Aug 2026 - 08:27 GMT Flu Vaccine - file CAIRO - 29 August 2026: The Egyptian Ministry of Health and Population announced the imminent availability of the seasonal influenza vaccine through the Serum and Vaccine Company (VACSERA), coinciding with the approach of winter and the start of the school year. This initiative aims to safeguard public health, protect citizens, and curb the spread of influenza viruses. The Ministry explained that the influenza virus is among the most widespread respiratory viruses, particularly among children in schools and in crowded places. This underscores the importance of vaccination as an effective preventative measure to reduce infection and complications. The Ministry affirmed its commitment to providing all types of influenza vaccines registered with the Egyptian Drug Authority, including trivalent and quadrivalent vaccines from all manufacturers, through VACSERA. This includes Korean, French, and American brands such as Sanofi, to meet public demand with a total supply exceeding 300,000 doses. The Ministry of Health and Population noted that VACSERA will begin distributing these vaccines gradually starting September 1st through its 28 centers located across most governorates of the country, ensuring service for citizens throughout Egypt. The Ministry of Health and Population affirmed that receiving the seasonal flu vaccine is a crucial step in boosting immunity throughout the winter season, emphasizing the importance of raising public awareness about how the vaccine works and the difference between preventing infection and reducing its severity. As part of its awareness campaign, "#TogetherWeAreReassured," the Ministry explained that the vaccine does not prevent infection with the virus 100%, but it significantly reduces the likelihood of infection. In the event of infection, symptoms are milder and less severe compared to unvaccinated individuals. Key points from the Ministry of Health regarding the flu vaccine: Reducing outbreaks and complications: Vaccination reduces the risk of pneumonia and the need for hospitalization or intensive care. Annual renewal of vaccination: The Ministry urges people to get vaccinated at the beginning of each season due to the constant change in virus strains. The Ministry emphasizes the necessity of vaccinating the elderly, those with chronic illnesses, children, pregnant women, and healthcare workers. The Ministry of Health and Population called on citizens to go to approved vaccination centers and hospitals to receive the vaccine, as part of the state’s tireless efforts to preserve the health and safety of society. 为什么即使你很健康也需要每年接种流感疫苗——西田纳西医疗保健

Why You Need a Flu Shot Every Year, Even If You’re Healthy - West Tennessee Healthcare
新闻简报西田纳西州医疗保健中心于 2026 年 8 月 28 日发表的文章强调了每年流感 疫苗接种 的重要性,即使对于健康个体也是如此。它解释了流感病毒不断进化,疫苗 每个季节都会更新,以更好地匹配流行毒株。2026-2027年流感季,美国流感疫苗中的所有三种病毒成分均较上一季进行了更新,其中一项变化专门针对2025-2026年流感季广泛传播的流感甲型H3N2流感病毒病毒。CDC 估计,在 2024 年至 2025 年季节期间,流感 疫苗接种 在美国避免了数百万例疾病和就诊,以及大约 180,000 例与流感相关的住院治疗。文章强调,即使对于健康的人来说,流感也可能导致严重的并发症,包括住院和慢性健康状况恶化。它建议 6 个月及以上的每个人每年进行一次 疫苗接种,除非有医疗原因需要避免,并建议应在 10 月底之前进行 疫苗接种,以确保在流感活动增加之前提供保护。文章还指出,流感疫苗是用灭活病毒或不含流感病毒制成的,不会引起流感。它承认没有 疫苗 提供完整的保护,但 疫苗接种 继续提供有意义的好处,例如降低疾病的严重程度和住院风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 8 月 28 日。2026-2027 年季节的流感 疫苗 更新基于 2025-2026 年季节流行的流感 甲型H3N2流感病毒 病毒。
地点与对象
这篇文章来自西田纳西州医疗保健中心,针对普通人群,包括健康个体、儿童、孕妇和患有慢性疾病的人。
事件
文章讨论了年度流感 疫苗接种 的重要性,强调流感病毒每年都会发生变化,并且 疫苗 也会更新以匹配流行毒株。它还强调,2026-2027年流感疫苗与上一季相比进行了更新,专门针对2025-2026年季节期间流行的流感甲型H3N2流感病毒病毒。
规模、影响与风险
CDC 估计,在 2024 年至 2025 年季节期间,流感 疫苗接种 在美国避免了数百万例疾病和就诊,以及大约 180,000 例与流感相关的住院治疗。文章指出,流感可能导致严重的并发症,包括住院和慢性健康状况恶化,即使对于健康的人也是如此。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文提供了有关流感 疫苗接种 的一般指南,包括接种时间、疫苗 类型以及谁应该接种疫苗的建议。它不报告任何具体的 暴发、病例数或调查状态。该信息基于 监测 数据和 疫苗 更新,未提及源中未解决的信息或限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Why You Need a Flu Shot Every Year, Even If You’re Healthy - West Tennessee Healthcare

News Brief
The article published by West Tennessee Healthcare on August 28, 2026, emphasizes the importance of annual flu vaccination, even for healthy individuals. It explains that influenza viruses evolve constantly, and vaccines are updated each season to better match the circulating strains. For the 2026–2027 flu season, all three virus components in U.S. flu vaccines were updated compared to the previous season, with one change specifically targeting the influenza A(H3N2) virus that circulated widely during the 2025–2026 season. The CDC estimates that during the 2024–2025 season, flu vaccination prevented millions of illnesses and medical visits, along with approximately 180,000 flu-related hospitalizations in the United States. The article highlights that flu can lead to serious complications, including hospitalization and worsening of chronic health conditions, even in healthy individuals. It recommends annual vaccination for everyone 6 months and older, unless there is a medical reason to avoid it, and suggests that vaccination should occur by the end of October to ensure protection before flu activity increases. The article also notes that flu shots are made with inactivated virus or without flu virus, and they cannot cause influenza. It acknowledges that no vaccine offers complete protection, but vaccination continues to offer meaningful benefits, such as reducing the severity of illness and the risk of hospitalization.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content My Health Records Hospital Charges Giving / Donating Search Generic filters Exact matches only Search Search Generic filters Exact matches only Find a Provider Search For Services Locations Careers Pay a Bill My Health Records Hospital Charges Giving / Donating Home / Why You Need a Flu Shot Every Year, Even If You’re Healthy August 28, 2026 Why You Need a Flu Shot Every Year, Even If You’re Healthy Primary Care , School Clinic , West Tennessee Medical Group You may be thinking, I rarely get sick, or I had a flu shot last year. Those are understandable reasons to wonder whether another vaccination is worth your time. The flu, however, changes from year to year, and so does the protection you need. An annual flu shot is one of the simplest steps you can take to reduce your risk of influenza and its potentially serious complications. It may also help make the illness less severe if you are vaccinated and still become sick. What Changed for the 2026–2027 Flu Season? For the 2026–2027 season, all three virus components in U.S. flu vaccines were updated compared with the previous season. One of those changes specifically addresses an influenza A(H3N2) virus that circulated widely during the 2025–2026 season. That update is an important reminder that last year’s vaccine is not designed for this year’s flu season. Scientists review surveillance data and update flu vaccines to better match the viruses expected to circulate. Why Do You Need a Flu Shot Every Year? Flu viruses keep changing Influenza viruses evolve constantly. The vaccine is reviewed and may be updated each season so it can target the strains experts expect to be most common. Protection decreases over time Your immune protection from vaccination can fade. Receiving a current seasonal vaccine helps refresh that protection when flu activity begins to rise. Flu can be more serious than people expect For some people, flu is more than a few uncomfortable days at home. It can lead to complications, hospitalization and worsening of chronic health conditions. Even healthy children and adults can develop serious illness. What Can a Flu Shot Do? Flu vaccine effectiveness varies from season to season, but vaccination continues to offer meaningful benefits. It can reduce flu illness and flu-related medical visits, lower the risk of hospitalization and help reduce the severity of illness among people who become infected despite vaccination. During the 2024–2025 season, the CDC estimates that flu vaccination prevented millions of illnesses and medical visits, along with approximately 180,000 flu-related hospitalizations in the United States. Those numbers change each year, but the purpose remains the same: reduce the likelihood that flu disrupts your health, work, school and family life. Who Should Get a Flu Vaccine? The CDC recommends annual flu vaccination for everyone 6 months and older who does not have a medical reason to avoid it. Your healthcare provider can help you choose an age-appropriate vaccine and discuss any concerns based on your health history. Vaccination is especially important for people who face a higher risk of serious flu complications, including older adults, young children, pregnant patients, people with weakened immune systems and people living with conditions such as asthma, diabetes, heart disease, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease or kidney disease. A note for families: Some children between 6 months and 8 years old need two flu vaccine doses at least four weeks apart. Your child’s pediatric or primary care provider can tell you how many doses are needed. When Is the Best Time to Get Vaccinated? For most people, September and October are good times to receive a flu vaccine. Ideally, vaccination should happen by the end of October, before flu activity typically increases. It takes about two weeks for your body to develop protection after vaccination. If October has already passed, it is not necessarily too late. Vaccination should continue throughout the season while flu viruses are circulating. Timing can be different for young children who need two doses, pregnant patients and adults 65 and older, so ask your provider what makes sense for you. Does Everyone Receive the Same Flu Vaccine? No. Several age-appropriate vaccine options may be available. Most people can receive any flu vaccine approved for their age and health status. Adults 65 and older are preferentially recommended to receive a high-dose, recombinant or adjuvanted flu vaccine when one is available. If one of those options is not available, receiving another age-appropriate flu vaccine is better than delaying vaccination. Pregnant patients should receive an injectable flu vaccine, not the nasal spray vaccine. The flu shot may be given during any trimester. The nasal spray is an option for some people ages 2 through 49, but it is not recommended during pregnancy or for people with certain medical conditions. Common Questions About the Flu Shot Can the flu shot give me the flu? No. Flu shots are made with inactivated virus or without flu virus, depending on the vaccine. They cannot cause influenza. Some people experience a sore arm, mild fever, headache, fatigue or muscle aches afterward. These effects are generally mild and short-lived. Can I still get the flu after vaccination? Yes. No vaccine offers complete protection, and effectiveness can vary depending on the viruses circulating that season. You also may be exposed before your body has had time to build protection. Even when vaccination does not prevent infection, it can still reduce the severity of illness. Should I talk with my provider first? A conversation is especially important if you have had a severe reaction to a flu vaccine, have a history of Guillain-Barré syndrome, are currently moderately or severely ill, are pregnant, or have questions about which vaccine is appropriate for your age or medical conditions. Make Flu Protection Part of Your Primary Care Plan A flu shot takes only a few minutes, but the decision can help protect your health throughout the season. Your primary care provider can review your vaccination history, answer questions and help you choose the vaccine that fits your age and health needs. Protect Yourself and Your Community Ready to get started? Schedule a flu vaccination with a West Tennessee Medical Group Primary Care clinic. Find a location near you. Osivax and GC Biopharma partner on broader flu vaccine - European Biotechnology Magazine

News Brief
On August 27, 2026, French biotech Osivax and South Korea-based GC Biopharma announced a partnership to develop a seasonal influenza vaccine. The collaboration combines Osivax’s broad-spectrum OVX836 candidate with GC Biopharma’s existing GC vaccine. This is a confirmed development effort, with no specific cases, deaths, or exposures reported. The scale and impact of the vaccine are not quantified in the evidence, and the risk level is not explicitly stated. The partnership is ongoing, and the vaccine development is in progress. No investigation, testing, or isolation measures are mentioned, indicating that the status is confirmed as a collaboration without uncertainty.

Osivax 和 GC Biopharma 建立战略合作,结合 OVX836 和 GC FLU 开发下一代流感 疫苗 - 马尼拉时报

Osivax and GC Biopharma Enter Strategic Collaboration to Develop a Next-Generation Influenza Vaccine Combining OVX836 and GC FLU - The Manila Times
新闻简报2026年8月27日,Osivax 和 GC Biopharma 宣布战略合作开发下一代 季节性流感 疫苗。疫苗 将 Osivax 的 OVX836(一种针对保守核蛋白(NP)抗原的 T 细胞 疫苗)与 GC Biopharma 的 GC FLU(一种经 WHO 资格预审的灭活 季节性流感 疫苗)相结合。这一伙伴关系旨在通过利用互补免疫机制来加强对不断演变的流感病毒株的保护。此次合作授予 Osivax 在日本和韩国境外使用 GC FLU 的非独家许可,GC Biopharma 保留在日本和韩国的独家权利。GC Biopharma 将提供特许权使用费并确保 GC FLU 的供应。该协议支持 Osivax 开发广谱 疫苗 以及 GC Biopharma 向先进市场的扩张。没有报告 暴发 或确诊病例,重点是 疫苗 的发展而不是活跃的健康事件。

时间
将 Osivax 的专有 OVX836 T 细胞 疫苗 和技术与 GC Biopharma 已上市的流感 疫苗 和全球制造专业知识相结合,将 Osivax 定位为差异化下一代流感 疫苗 候选药物的全球开发领导者,旨在针对法国里昂和韩国龙仁的高度突变 季节性流感 菌株提供增强且更持久的保护 - 2026 年 8 月 27 日 - Osivax 是一家开发差异化流感 疫苗 的临床阶段生物制药公司,旨在产生广谱 免疫应答,GC Biopharma 是一家在 疫苗 和血浆衍生产品方面拥有丰富经验的韩国领先生物制药公司,今天宣布建立长期合作,开发和商业化下一代 季节性流感 疫苗 候选药物旨在针对多种流感病毒株提供更广泛、更持久的保护。

法国里昂和韩国龙仁;针对影响人类的 季节性流感 菌株。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Osivax and GC Biopharma Enter Strategic Collaboration to Develop a Next-Generation Influenza Vaccine Combining OVX836 and GC FLU - The Manila Times

News Brief
On August 27, 2026, Osivax and GC Biopharma announced a strategic collaboration to develop a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine. The vaccine combines Osivax's OVX836, a T-cell vaccine targeting the conserved nucleoprotein (NP) antigen, with GC Biopharma's GC FLU, a WHO-prequalified inactivated seasonal influenza vaccine. This partnership aims to enhance protection against evolving influenza strains by leveraging complementary immune mechanisms. The collaboration grants Osivax a non-exclusive license to use GC FLU outside Japan and Korea, where GC Biopharma retains exclusive rights. GC Biopharma will provide royalties and ensure supply of GC FLU. The agreement supports Osivax's development of a broad-spectrum vaccine and GC Biopharma's expansion into advanced markets. No outbreak or confirmed cases are reported, and the focus is on vaccine development rather than an active health event.
Original source text (English)
Combines Osivax's proprietary OVX836 T-cell vaccine and technology with GC Biopharma's marketed influenza vaccine and worldwide manufacturing expertise Positions Osivax as the global development lead for a differentiated next-generation influenza vaccine candidate Aims to deliver enhanced and more durable protection against highly mutating seasonal influenza strains Lyon, France and Yongin, Republic of Korea - 27 August 2026 - Osivax , a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company developing differentiated influenza vaccines designed to generate broad-spectrum immune responses, and GC Biopharma, a leading South Korean biopharmaceutical company with extensive experience in vaccines and plasma-derived products, today announced a long-term collaboration to develop and commercialize a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine candidate designed to provide broader and more durable protection against a wide range of influenza strains. Under the agreement, the companies will develop the combination of OVX836, Osivax's broad-spectrum influenza A vaccine candidate targeting the highly conserved nucleoprotein (NP), with GC FLU, GC Biopharma's standard-dose, WHO-prequalified, inactivated seasonal influenza vaccine, in a single injection. The combination is expected to leverage complementary immune mechanisms to provide higher and broader protection against circulating and emerging influenza strains. OVX836 is designed to induce T-cell responses against the highly conserved NP antigen, while GC FLU provides antibody responses against the well-known Hemagglutinin (HA) surface antigen. The agreement grants Osivax a non-exclusive, sublicensable, global license (excluding Japan and Korea) to use GC FLU in combination with OVX836. Osivax will lead the global development strategy for the combination vaccine candidate and will be responsible for its development and commercialization outside the Republic of Korea, where GC Biopharma will retain exclusive rights. GC Biopharma will receive royalties and ensure long-term supply of GC FLU. Additional details and financial terms have not been disclosed. "Despite decades of progress in influenza vaccination, there remains a significant need for approaches that can provide broader and more durable protection against evolving influenza strains,” said Alexandre Le Vert, CEO and Co-Founder of Osivax . "This agreement marks a key step towards developing a superior seasonal influenza vaccine by combining Osivax’s T-cell-focused approach with GC Biopharma’s established GC FLU vaccine and manufacturing capabilities. This partnership is a cornerstone of our strategy to lead the development of a next-generation seasonal influenza vaccine while preserving Osivax’s strategic independence.” Get the latest news delivered to your inbox Sign up for The Manila Times newsletters By signing up with an email address, I acknowledge that I have read and agree to the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy . "Advancing next-generation influenza vaccines requires collaboration between companies with complementary expertise across various scientific disciplines," said Eun-Chul Huh, CEO of GC Biopharma . "We are pleased to partner with Osivax to develop a combination vaccine candidate designed to address long-standing unmet needs in seasonal influenza prevention. This agreement supports GC Biopharma's continued expansion into major advanced markets while reinforcing our domestic position." Seasonal influenza remains a significant public health burden, particularly among older adults and people at increased risk of complications. Current seasonal influenza vaccines primarily target surface antigens that can vary substantially from season to season. OVX836 is designed to target NP, a more conserved internal influenza A antigen, with the aim of broadening protection across influenza A strains. Osivax previously reported that co-administration of OVX836 with a seasonal inactivated influenza vaccine was well tolerated and induced complementary immune responses, supporting further development of combination approaches. About OVX836 OVX836 is a first-in-class influenza A vaccine candidate that targets the nucleoprotein (NP), a highly conserved internal antigen. Unlike surface antigens, the NP is much less likely to mutate, providing a broader and more universal immune response. Osivax’ oligoDOMTM technology enables the design and production of a recombinant version of the NP, which self-assembles into a nanoparticle, thus triggering powerful T- and B-cell immune responses. OVX836 has been tested in 7 clinical trials with over 4,000 participants so far, and has shown promising safety, immunogenicity, and efficacy read-outs. About GC FLU GC FLU is GC Biopharma's seasonal influenza vaccine. WHO prequalification listings identify GC FLU inj. and GC FLU Multi inj. as current WHO-prequalified seasonal trivalent influenza vaccines manufactured by GC Biopharma Corp. in the Republic of Korea. About Osivax Osivax is a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company developing a portfolio of differentiated influenza vaccines generating superior T-cell responses in addition to strong and sustained B-cell responses, thanks to its novel, self-assembling nanoparticle platform technology, oligoDOMTM. The company’s lead candidate, OVX836, is a broad-spectrum influenza A vaccine candidate currently in Phase 2b clinical trials with over 4,000 volunteers tested and encouraging efficacy proof of concept data. By combining OVX836 with standard flu vaccines, Osivax is advancing toward a best-in-class seasonal flu vaccine to prevent all strains of influenza. Osivax collaborates with leading global health agencies, including US based BARDA and NIH, and European-based institutions and top-tier pharmaceutical companies, reinforcing its mission to prevent all types of seasonal and pandemic influenza. The company will expand into other infectious disease indications through combinations and collaborations worldwide. For further information: www.osivax.com About GC Biopharma GC Biopharma, formerly known as Green Cross Corporation, is a biopharmaceutical company headquartered in Yongin, Republic of Korea. The company has more than half a century of experience in the development and manufacturing of plasma derivatives and vaccines, and is expanding its global presence. GC Biopharma also continues to invest in innovation by leveraging R&D capabilities in protein engineering, mRNA and lipid nanoparticle drug delivery technologies to develop therapeutics in areas including rare diseases and immunology and inflammation. H1N1 流感病例:“无需恐慌” - Rediff

H1N1, Flu Cases: 'No Need To Panic' - Rediff
新闻简报多个印度城市报告流感病例激增,多地邦政府已发布公共场所佩戴口罩与加强呼吸道卫生的建议。印度医学研究理事会(ICMR)确认当前疫情上升为季节性流感模式,未发现新型毒株。在德里,3000例流感病例中超过2300例为猪流感(H1N1),卡纳塔克邦则报告约4000例病例。2026年7月,抗感染药物销售额同比增长6.15%,达2470亿卢比;抗病毒药物销售额增长25.5%,达109.2亿卢比。多数病例为轻症且可自限,住院病例有限。医生指出,大多数患者仅需门诊管理,抗病毒药物药物建议用于孕妇或合并症患者。该病毒为自2009年大流行以来流行的熟悉甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09谱系。
